### [1] [The noise cost of reservoir locality in broadband quantum amplifier chains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20654)
*Chengxi Li,Hao Zhu,Tie-Fu Zhang,Lin Zhuang,Wanzi Sun,Wuming Liu*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: 本文研究了定向量子放大器中有限带宽噪声抑制问题,通过构建相邻位点损耗通道达到局部兼容条件下的单频最小值基准,并对均匀链和二聚化链在不同储库配置下的噪声性能进行了系统比较,为低温微波阵列的储库设计和噪声校准提供了理论指导。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 定向量子放大器在实现弱信号读出的同时保护信号源,但储库噪声限制了灵敏度。有限带宽抑制必须协调频率依赖响应与固定的局部耦合和有限速率之间的矛盾。现有研究缺乏在局部兼容性约束下对多位点系统噪声性能的系统性理论基准和优化策略,特别是对于实际低温微波阵列应用所需的有限带宽场景。

Method: 构建满足局部兼容性条件的相邻位点损耗通道,作为达到已知单频最小值的无上限理论基准。针对指定的均匀链和二聚化链结构,在匹配漂移、端口、高增益带宽以及完整增益/损耗速率上限条件下,比较固定储库的性能。通过边界响应界限确定必要的耦合速率和需要二阶近邻范围的目标。使用有限辅助计算方法在匹配总耦合预算下评估噪声优势,并通过响应界限量化噪声-分辨率容差。

Result: 在代表性参数(速率上限R=8,半宽Ω=0.05)下,对于所有有限N≥64的均匀链,最近邻算术带平均噪声比无限制最优值高出超过1.01265个量子。在N=64时,有限辅助配置在匹配总耦合预算下仍保持噪声优势。边界响应界限给出了需要二阶近邻范围的必要速率和目标参数。理论计算量化了噪声-分辨率容差范围。

Conclusion: 研究建立了局部兼容性约束下量子放大器有限带宽噪声抑制的理论基准,揭示了最近邻耦合配置的本质噪声劣势,证明了扩展耦合范围和优化储库设计的必要性。这些理论结果为设计带宽100 kHz的低温微波阵列提供了储库设计和噪声校准的定量指导,对实际量子测量系统的优化具有重要应用价值。

Abstract: Directional quantum amplifiers enable weak-signal readout while protecting the source, but reservoir noise limits sensitivity. Finite-band suppression must reconcile frequency-dependent responses with fixed local couplings and finite rates. We construct adjacent-site loss channels attaining the known single-frequency minimum under local compatibility conditions, as an uncapped benchmark. For specified uniform and dimerized chains, we compare fixed reservoirs at matched drift, ports, high-gain band and complete gain/loss rate caps. At the representative cap $R=8$ and halfwidth $Ω=0.05$, nearest-neighbor arithmetic band average noise exceeds the unrestricted optimum by more than $1.01265$ quanta in the uniform chain for every finite $N\ge64$. Boundary-response bounds give necessary rates and targets requiring range two. At $N=64$, finite-auxiliary calculations retain a noise advantage under matched total coupling budgets; response bounds quantify noise-resolution tolerances. These theoretical results guide reservoir design and noise calibration for a proposed cryogenic microwave array with a $100$ kHz design band.

</details>


### [2] [Mechanical trisqueezing in circuit quantum acoustodynamics with SNAIL devices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20670)
*Yi-Zhou Bu,Matteo Fadel,Kai Xu,Heng Fan*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: 本文提出了一种电路量子声动力学(cQAD)架构,通过将机械振荡器与超导非线性非对称电感元件(SNAIL)耦合,利用三波混频相互作用生成三压缩态,实现了机械量子系统中真正的非高斯演化,为通用连续变量量子计算提供了硬件高效的实现路径。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 连续变量量子信息处理需要包含真正非高斯演化的通用门集合。现有的电路量子声动力学实验虽然实现了双线性和四次(四波混频)操作,但由于transmon量子比特固有的Kerr非线性,无法获得奇数阶有效哈密顿量(如三压缩)。因此需要开发新的架构来实现机械振荡器的非高斯资源生成,推进通用连续变量量子计算和量子传感技术的发展。

Method: 提出了一种新型cQAD架构:将高泛音体声波谐振器(HBAR)中的机械模式与超导非线性非对称电感元件(SNAIL)耦合。通过结合SNAIL的三次非线性和参数化磁通驱动,设计出三波混频相互作用。从完整的电路模型推导出有效的三压缩哈密顿量,确定了隔离所需相互作用(排除竞争项)所需的驱动频率和磁通调制,并针对当前制造能力兼容的参数量化了可实现的耦合强度。

Result: 成功设计出能够生成三压缩机械态的方案,该态具有特征性的三角形星状Wigner函数,其负值区域表明了非高斯性。理论分析表明该方案在当前制造技术条件下可实现,能够获得足够的耦合强度来产生明显的三压缩效应。

Conclusion: 本研究建立了一条硬件高效的路径,用于在机械量子系统中生成非高斯资源。该方案扩展了电路量子声动力学的工具箱,为实现通用连续变量量子计算和增强的量子极限传感开辟了新途径。通过利用SNAIL的奇数阶非线性,克服了传统方案的局限性,实现了之前无法获得的三压缩相互作用。

Abstract: Continuous-variable quantum information processing with mechanical oscillators requires a uni- versal gate set, including a genuine non-Gaussian evolution. While circuit quantum acoustodynamics (cQAD) experiments have realized bilinear and quartic (four-wave mixing) operations by coupling mechanical modes to transmon qubits, the intrinsic Kerr nonlinearity of these devices precludes access to odd-order effective Hamiltonians such as trisqueezing. Here we propose a cQAD architec- ture in which a mechanical mode, hosted in a high-overtone bulk acoustic-wave resonator (HBAR), is coupled to a superconducting nonlinear asymmetric inductive element (SNAIL). By combining the cubic nonlinearity of the SNAIL with parametric flux driving, we engineer a three-wave mixing interaction that generates trisqueezed mechanical states, producing the characteristic triangular, star-shaped Wigner function with negative regions indicative of non-Gaussianity. We derive the effective trisqueezing Hamiltonian from the full circuit model, identify the drive frequencies and flux modulation required to isolate the desired interaction from competing terms, and quantify the achievable coupling strengths for parameters compatible with current fabrication capabilities. Our results establish a hardware-efficient route to non-Gaussian resource generation in mechanical quantum systems, extending the toolbox of cQAD toward universal continuous-variable quantum computation and enhanced quantum-limited sensing.

</details>


### [3] [Quantum Partial Information Decompositions, an Operational Order on Ensembles, and Direct Quantifications of Objective Information](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20694)
*Alexander Nico-Katz*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: 本文将部分信息分解(PID)框架扩展到多方量子系统,构建了两种基于Holevo量的量子PID,并通过客观性场景中的扩展图态验证了其应用价值,为系统构建通用多方量子PID奠定了基础。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 经典部分信息分解框架能够系统分析多方系统中的信息结构,但现有量子PID扩展仅限于二方系统。为了在量子信息理论中实现对多方系统信息结构的精细刻画,需要将PID框架推广到通用的多方量子场景,并建立其在量子信息问题中的实际应用方法。

Method: 本文提出了构建多方量子PID的简单扩展框架。首先,基于经典最小互信息构建了朴素的Holevo量子PID。其次,通过发展量子系综可转换性的操作性偏序关系,构建了精细的Holevo量子PID。最后,将所提出的量子PID应用于客观性场景分析,使用Holevo QPID直接量化扩展图态中的客观信息。

Result: 成功构建了两种基于Holevo量的多方量子PID:一种是基于经典最小互信息的朴素版本,另一种是基于系综可转换性操作偏序的精细版本。在扩展图态的客观性场景中验证了这些QPID的有效性,能够直接量化其中的客观信息。建立了量子系综上的操作性偏序关系,为量子PID的系统构建提供了理论工具。

Conclusion: 本研究为系统构建通用多方量子PID建立了坚实基础,引入了量子系综上具有操作意义的偏序关系,推进了量子PID作为独立研究对象的理论价值,并展示了其在量子信息问题(如量子客观性分析)中的直接应用潜力。这些成果将量子信息分解理论从二方系统扩展到了多方系统,为量子信息结构的精细分析提供了新的数学工具。

Abstract: The partial information decomposition (PID) framework is an extension of classical information theory that enables a systematic analysis of the structure of information in multipartite systems. We advance a simple extension of this framework enabling the construction of multipartite quantum PIDs (QPIDs); generalizing recent, exclusively bipartite, extensions. We construct two QPIDs of the Holevo quantity. First a naive QPID based on the classical minimum mutual information. Second a sophisticated QPID enabled by our development of an operational partial order on quantum ensembles in terms of ensemble (inter-)convertability. Finally, as a concrete example of how QPIDs can be applied, we investigate objectivity scenarios; using our Holevo QPIDs to directly quantify objective information in extended graph states. These findings ultimately establish a foundation upon which generic multipartite QPIDs can be systematically constructed, introduce an operational partial order on ensembles, advance QPIDs as interesting objects of study in their own right, and demonstrate their immediate applicability to problems in quantum information.

</details>


### [4] [Surviving readout errors and incomplete postselection in monitored quantum circuits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20712)
*Murtaza Ali Mir,Teemu Ojanen,Ali G. Moghaddam*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: 本文研究了读出误差对监测量子电路动力学的影响,发现U(1)对称电路中的电荷锐化相变在有限误差率下仍然存在,通过分解量子和经典涨落贡献,提出了一种最小缓解框架来定位相变并从实验数据中提取相变信号。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 中程测量的读出误差比标准的电路末端测量更难以缓解,因为误差会破坏后选择过程本身。现有研究缺乏对监测量子电路中读出误差如何影响测量诱导相变(如电荷锐化相变)的系统理解,需要探索在噪声环境下相变的存活性及其实验可探测特征。

Method: 聚焦于U(1)对称量子电路,研究不完全后选择(由读出误差引起)对电荷涨落动力学的影响。将电荷涨落分解为量子贡献和经典贡献两部分,分析不同测量率下涨落的行为特征。通过研究涨落随测量率的变化关系,识别理想相变点附近的浅极小值,该极小值反映了测量诱导纯化与噪声诱导混合之间的竞争。

Result: 电荷锐化相变在有限读出误差率下仍然存在,但锐化相中的零涨落被有限稳态平台取代。电荷涨落在理想相变点附近作为测量率的函数呈现浅极小值,标志着测量诱导纯化和噪声诱导混合的竞争。这个极小值出现的位置表明不完全后选择仍保留足够的轨迹记忆来衰减噪声混合,在噪声环境中留下相变的可探测信号。量子-经典涨落分解提供了一种最小缓解框架,可直接从实验数据中定位相变。

Conclusion: 研究证明电荷锐化相变在读出误差存在下以定量且实验可及的方式存活。通过将涨落分解为量子和经典成分,提出了一种针对中程测量读出误差的最小缓解策略,为从含噪实验数据中直接提取测量诱导相变信息提供了可行路径。这为理解和实验验证噪声环境下的监测量子电路相变提供了重要理论指导。

Abstract: We study how readout errors affect dynamics in monitored quantum circuits. Such errors make postselection effectively incomplete, conditioning trajectories on imperfect rather than true records. Focusing on U(1)-symmetric circuits, we find that the charge-sharpening transition survives at a finite error rate, with the sharp phase's vanishing fluctuations replaced by a finite steady-state plateau. The charge fluctuations show a shallow minimum near the ideal transition point as a function of measurement rate, signaling the competition between measurement-induced purification and noise-induced mixing. By decomposing the fluctuations into quantum and classical contributions, we show the minimum marks where incomplete postselection still retains enough trajectory memory to attenuate noise-induced mixing, leaving a detectable signature of the transition even in a noisy setting. Mid-circuit measurements are far harder to mitigate against readout errors than standard end-of-circuit ones, because the errors corrupt the postselection itself; the decomposition therefore offers a minimal mitigation framework and a route to locate the transition directly from experimental data. Our results show that charge sharpening survives readout errors in a quantitative and experimentally accessible way.

</details>

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 准备-测量场景是量子通信中最简单且应用最广泛的设置之一，尽管结构简单，但能够揭示经典与量子通信的本质差异、展现量子优势，并在量子密码学和设备认证中发挥重要作用。然而，该领域缺乏系统性的入门教程，使得新研究者难以掌握相关概念和技术工具。因此，本文旨在为初学者提供一个从基础到应用的全面介绍，帮助他们理解现有文献并将准备-测量方法应用于自己的研究。

Method: 本文采用教程式的系统介绍方法，主要包括：(1) 从零开始介绍准备-测量场景的基本概念和结构；(2) 阐述用于约束和表征通信的不同框架，特别强调半设备无关方法在量子信息中的应用；(3) 详细讲解现代凸优化技术用于表征这些场景中可实现的关联；(4) 通过实例演示如何在实践中应用这些工具；(5) 讨论开放问题和未来研究方向。

Result: 本文成功构建了一个完整的准备-测量场景知识体系，涵盖了从基础理论到实践应用的各个层面。文章系统地介绍了不同的理论框架，特别是半设备无关方法，并展示了凸优化技术在分析量子关联中的有效性。通过具体实例，展示了这些方法在量子密码学和设备认证等实际问题中的应用价值，为研究者提供了可操作的技术工具和分析方法。

Conclusion: 本教程为准备-测量场景提供了全面而系统的介绍，使初学者能够掌握该领域的核心概念和技术方法。通过强调半设备无关方法和凸优化技术，文章为研究者提供了分析量子通信优势和开发实用协议的有力工具。文章最后指出了若干开放问题和未来研究方向，表明准备-测量场景仍然是一个活跃且富有潜力的研究领域，在量子信息理论和应用中具有持续的重要性。

Abstract: Prepare-and-measure scenarios are among the simplest and most widely used settings for quantum communication. A sender prepares a physical system in one of several possible states, which a receiver then measures. Despite this simple structure, these scenarios offer a rich framework for exploring the differences between classical and quantum communication, revealing quantum advantages, and developing protocols for quantum cryptography and device certification. In this tutorial, we give a ground-up introduction to prepare-and-measure scenarios and the methods used to study them. We introduce different frameworks for constraining and characterising communication, with an emphasis on semi-device-independent approaches to quantum information. We also cover modern convex-optimisation techniques for characterising the correlations achievable in these scenarios and illustrate how these tools can be applied in practice. Our aim is to give newcomers the conceptual and technical foundations needed to approach the current literature and apply prepare-and-measure methods in their own research. We conclude with a discussion of open problems and directions for future work.

</details>

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 薛定谔猫态是量子信息处理和量子增强测量的重要非高斯资源。尽管从压缩真空态中减除光子已被证明可用于在光学域中生成此类态，但在保持高保真度和高预报概率的同时扩展到大振幅仍然具有挑战性。现有的单分支优化方法未能充分利用实验资源，需要开发更有效的生成策略来克服这些障碍。

Method: 本文提出交替压缩和光子数减除操作的方案，并将猫态生成问题重新表述为保真度约束下的多分支优化问题。该方法不针对单一预报结果进行优化，而是在一个共同的实验设置下，联合优化所有满足规定保真度阈值的光子数结果的总概率。研究推导了条件态、猫态保真度和预报概率的解析表达式，考虑了可用压缩资源和光子数分辨能力的实验约束，系统分析了不同架构配置下的资源权衡、对实验参数偏差的敏感性以及预报损耗。

Result: 在无光子数分辨限制时，最优的交替方案架构简化为两种单次减除端点配置之一，具体取决于可用的压缩资源。当光子数分辨能力受到额外限制时，真正的交替分支方案变得更有优势。相比传统的单次光子减除方案,该方法在保真度和成功概率方面实现了改进，且不一定需要增加压缩资源。多分支优化提供了更具操作性的可用生成概率度量,可以支持与单分支最优值不同的参数配置。

Conclusion: 本文建立了一套系统的多分支优化框架用于薛定谔猫态的生成，通过解析表达式实现了高效优化和资源权衡分析。研究结果表明，根据不同的资源预算（压缩强度和光子数分辨能力），可以确定最适合的架构配置。多分支联合优化策略相比传统单分支方法能够更有效地利用实验资源，为实际量子信息处理和量子测量应用中高质量猫态的可扩展生成提供了指导。

Abstract: Schrödinger cat states are important non-Gaussian resources for quantum information processing and quantum-enhanced metrology. Although photon subtraction from squeezed vacuum states has proven useful for generating such states in the optical domain, scaling to large amplitudes while maintaining high fidelity and heralding probability remains challenging. To address these obstacles, we analyze alternating squeezing and photon-number-subtraction operations. Rather than optimizing for a single heralding outcome, we formulate cat-state generation as a fidelity-constrained multibranch problem, jointly optimizing the total probability of all photon-number outcomes satisfying a prescribed fidelity threshold at one common experimental setting. Already for plain photon subtraction, this provides a more operational measure of the usable generation probability and can favor parameter configurations that differ from single-branch optima. Considering experimental constraints on available squeezing and photon-number-resolving capability, we demonstrate improvements in fidelity and success probability compared with plain photon subtraction, without necessarily increasing the required squeezing resources. Without restrictions on photon-number resolution, we find that the optimal architecture of the alternating scheme reduces to one of two single-subtraction endpoint configurations, depending on the available squeezing resources. When photon-number resolution is additionally limited, genuinely alternating branches can become advantageous. We derive analytical expressions for the conditional states, cat-state fidelities, and heralding probabilities, enabling efficient multibranch optimization and a systematic analysis of resource trade-offs, sensitivity to experimental parameter deviations, and heralding loss. Together, these results identify the architecture best suited to a given resource budget.

</details>

<details>

Abstract: Developing mechanisms to control transport in correlated quantum systems is essential for advancing quantum circuit technologies. Once a bias between the baths is introduced, the drive-induced current contribution can reinforce, compete with, or even cancel the bias-induced contribution. These effects enable rectification in the non-interacting regime, which we quantify via the rectification coefficient $\mathcal{R}$. This coefficient measures the asymmetry between transport up the tilt (forward bias) and down the tilt (reverse bias). In addition to standard rectification, where transport is reduced under reverse bias, we identify a regime of bipolar rectification, in which the current flows only up the tilt regardless of the bias polarity. At the boundary between standard and bipolar regimes, the system achieves perfect rectification, corresponding to complete suppression of the reverse-bias current. In the presence of interactions between particles, the parameter region that supports bipolar rectification is enlarged and $\mathcal{R}$ can be enhanced by many orders of magnitude. Our results establish a rectification mechanism for particle and energy transport that can be experimentally realized in condensed matter and cold atom systems.

We also settle the two open links of their inequality chain: the graph-level no-signalling simulation rate always equals its single-letter value $\log_2Σ(G)$, and a 32-vertex graph from the Mermin-Peres magic square has $C_{\min}<\log_2Σ$, certified in exact rational arithmetic. As an explicit example, we prove that for stabilizer systems with short-range order, the local algebras are isomorphic to tensor products of hyperfinite factors of type I or type II. This demonstrates that the absence of magic is accompanied by the absence of type III factors in the local algebras, therefore prohibiting the appearance of a quantum field theory. Second, for the hyperfinite factors of Araki, Woods and Powers, we prove a quantitative equivalence: The limiting factor is of type III if and only if the locally optimized min-relative entropy of magic diverges. Abstract: Quantum imaginary-time evolution (QITE) provides a systematic route to ground-state preparation, but its cost grows rapidly with the locality $D$ of the unitary approximation, for which the local Pauli basis scales as $4^D$. We introduce Sparse-QITE, a regularized formulation that promotes compact generators, reducing the number of quantum operations and yielding circuits with fewer gates and lower depth. We derive rigorous bounds on the sparsification error and benchmark the method in one-dimensional transverse-field Ising and antiferromagnetic Heisenberg models. Sparse-QITE exhibits a controllable sparsity--accuracy trade-off while retaining strong compression even in the high-accuracy regime. In scalability experiments with respect to $D$, the active support grows much more slowly than in standard QITE and, in several regimes, approximately saturates over the domain sizes studied. After circuit construction and transpilation, some of the largest-domain cases require only about $1\%$ of the standard-QITE gate count while maintaining high accuracy. We further introduce adaptive and active-discovery variants: the former selects the regularization strength dynamically during the evolution, while the latter reduces the number of Pauli expectation values that need to be evaluated and, consequently, the measurement requirements. These results establish sparsity-promoting regularization as a route toward more resource-efficient QITE. More broadly, these results suggest that sparsity-promoting regularization may also benefit variational quantum algorithms.

Abstract: Determining whether quantum states can be interconverted by a prescribed set of transformations is a fundamental problem in quantum information. Here we develop a general classification framework based on conserved operators (COs), i.e., not necessarily hermitian matrices having the state of interest as an eigenstate. We first show that COs belonging to the algebra generating the allowed transformations can be obtained from the kernel of a quantum covariance matrix. Their number determines the dimension of the accessible orbit, while their algebraic structure and eigenvalues provide further constraints on state convertibility. We establish general bounds on the number of COs and prove that highest-weight states, like separable states in the case of $n$ qudits, attain the maximum. We also show how these operators generate parent Hamiltonians having the characterized state as an exact ground state. We then also consider positive zero-eigenvalue COs, which encode forbidden directions of reduced density matrices and incorporate rank-based constraints into the same framework. Applying this approach to multiqubit SLOCC, we obtain complementary classifications from algebraic and rank COs. While for $n\leq 3$ qubits both lead to the usual classes, for $n=4$ qubits they distinguish 17 and 20 types separately, and 29 when combined. The combined classification yields 287 fixed types and $4$ continuous families for $n=5$ qubits, and 4926 fixed types and 147 continuous families for $n=6$ qubits, providing, to our knowledge, the most refined exhaustive classification of SLOCC-convertibility classes based on such data.

  <summary>Details</summary>

Result: 数值实验验证了理论预测：对于固定数量的算符项，增加系统位点数量确实能加速计算。monoprop框架展现出近乎理想的并行扩展性，可扩展至8192个处理器核心，成功追踪了9.5×10¹⁰个算符项。该框架实现了显著的性能提升，并为大规模量子化学模拟和自适应量子线路优化提供了高效工具。

Conclusion: monoprop框架通过反对易索引和局域性优化，实现了算符传播计算复杂度的突破性降低（从O(KN)到O(K/N)），揭示了一个反直觉的结论：增加系统规模反而能提高计算效率。该开源框架具有出色的大规模并行性能，结合替代图等创新技术，为量子化学模拟和变分量子算法的实际应用提供了强大而高效的计算工具，有望推动大规模量子模拟研究的发展。

Abstract: We introduce monoprop, an open-source, massively parallel operator-propagation framework for fermion and qubit bases. For $N$ qubits or modes, existing codes represent each of the $K$ tracked operator terms (Pauli strings or products of Majorana operators) as a $2N$-bit string, testing each for anticommutation with the gate at $O(KN)$ cost per gate. Here, we introduce the anticommutation index which rapidly looks up the terms anticommuting with a gate. A local gate changes only the terms that touch its sites, and when terms are truncated to act on few sites, only a fraction $\sim1/N$ touch any given qubit or mode, and we use this to prove the time per gate scales as $O(K/N)$. This counterintuitive result tells us that for a fixed number of terms, increasing the number of sites can make things faster. We demonstrate this numerically, and show monoprop scales almost ideally to 8192 cores, tracking $9.5\times10^{10}$ terms. Beyond propagation, we present memory-efficient surrogate graphs, which record one propagation and replay it at new angles, for fast energy re-estimation and parameter gradients, and an efficient method for finding energy-minimising gates and optimal angles for simulating adaptive quantum-chemistry circuits.

</details>

Abstract: Tests of gravity-mediated entanglement aim to determine whether two massive objects in spatial superposition can become entangled through their mutual gravitational interaction. We find finite parameter windows where the same state is Bell-nonlocal and teleportation-useful, but these windows shrink as the wave packets become broader or the dephasing becomes stronger. The results identify finite-width and electrostatic tolerance boundaries that should be considered in future tests of gravity-mediated entanglement.

Abstract: Recent studies have reported promising results from quantum machine learning in drug discovery, healthcare, materials science and cybersecurity. Research includes comparisons with classical machine learning algorithms and the development of sequential hybrid pipelines. We propose that fusion can improve prediction when classical and quantum representations capture complementary predictive relationships. Our classical-quantum multi-view framework preserves the full classical input alongside simulated quantum features. Concatenation and attention combine the views; single-view comparisons, replacement and masking controls assess both views' contributions. Controlled angular-polynomial experiments distinguish targets adequately represented by a single view from those that depend on complementary angular and polynomial relationships. For these mixed targets, fusion improves prediction by combining the relevant feature sets, while matched classical representations recover the same benefit, linking the improvement to their complementary predictive content. Controlled classification tests further show that gains depend on alignment between the chosen representation and the target, and on a suitable fusion rule. Benchmark validation shows improved mean predictive performance over classical baselines on Wine, Wisconsin Diagnostic Breast Cancer, three-class Fashion-MNIST and Steel Plates Faults within the evaluated protocols. Classical replacement and masking controls remain competitive, classical baselines lead on Digits and Covertype, and outcomes with missing or corrupted views depend on the available inputs. These findings support evaluating quantum representations for their complementary predictive contribution and matching the representation and fusion strategy to the target relationships.

Abstract: Long-range, genuine multipartite entanglement (GME) between distant parties in a quantum network is a crucial resource for distributed quantum information processing. Through controlled manipulation of short-range GME states in the network via stochastic local operations aided by classical communication (SLOCC), long-range GME can be established between selected target nodes on demand. Optimizing such multipartite entanglement distribution protocols under constraints imposed by the network architecture, target-node set, and available resource budget is a key challenge in resource-efficient manipulation of GME. Applying our approach to networks of Dür-Cirac-Tarrach states reveals a rich landscape of achievable end-to-end GME and accessible entanglement classes. More generally, our framework provides a systematic approach to the construction and optimization of multipartite quantum repeater protocols for scalable and resource-efficient GME distribution in quantum networks.

These results establish an asymmetric indium architecture combined with multi angle compression as a robust, coherence preserving route toward 3D integrated superconducting quantum processors.

Abstract: We investigate the Casimir force on thin suspended dielectric layers covered with graphene, both theoretically and experimentally. Thermal effects of the Casimir force on graphene occur at separations significantly smaller compared to ordinary metals and dielectrics. At such small separations, the Casimir force is sufficiently strong to be measured experimentally. However, the presence of substrates to support the graphene significantly reduces the enhancement. We show theoretically that replacing the solid substrate beneath graphene with a thin dielectric membrane partially restores this strong thermal dependence. One of the interacting objects is a solid gold plate. The other object is a suspended dielectric membrane made of either 15 nm of silicon oxide or 13 nm silicon nitride, covered with graphene. At a separation of $\sim$500 nm, theoretical predictions yield changes in the Casimir pressure of up to 54 % and 40 % for silicon oxide and silicon nitride, respectively, when the temperature is increased from 10 K to 294 K. Measurements were performed for graphene on a suspended silicon nitride membrane at room temperature, using a gold-coated sphere attached to a cantilever as the force sensor. The measured force gradient is consistent with the theoretical prediction, although the thermal dependence could not be resolved, in part because of uncertainties in the optical properties of silicon nitride. Replacing a bulk substrate with a thin dielectric membrane covered with graphene provides a substantial reduction of the Casimir force, which may facilitate the stable operation of neutral nanomechanical elements at small separations.

Using a three-dimensional photonic chip, we demonstrate on-demand state engineering through dark-mode preservation and bright-mode selection, enabling the protection of topological corner states and excited-state shelving. We further observe the predicted transition of the total detection probability on an effectively infinite one-dimensional lattice and identify its microscopic origin in Floquet near-dark modes. These results establish periodic conditional measurements as a general framework for engineering conditional quantum states and manipulating event-detection statistics in monitored quantum dynamics.

Each public message may be an affine function of the sender's raw key, chosen adaptively from the public history. For every finite input length and every accepted transcript of positive probability, Eve's optimal probability of recovering any affine function of Alice's raw key is at least Bob's. Consequently, reliable agreement on a linear final key is incompatible with secrecy. The same conclusion holds for arbitrary publicly seeded privacy amplification after reliable recovery of a linear candidate. The proof conditions on the complete transcript, which restricts the two raw keys to affine subspaces. The result allows block splitting, regrouping, nonzero-syndrome selection, and adaptive linear reconciliation.

Abstract: We consider the quantum Rabi model, a fundamental model of quantum optics describing a two-level system coupled to a bosonic mode, including counter-rotating terms. Although the model is exactly solvable, simple analytic expressions for important physical quantities are generally unavailable. A sparsity penalty encourages the removal of unnecessary phases, while temperature annealing gradually turns the masks into binary decisions. We compare the proposed method with fully parameterized and predefined sparse ansatzes on Fourier and Haar-random target states. The evaluation considers both the minimum number of phases required to reach different infidelity thresholds and the overall trade-off between fidelity and phases required, measured through Pareto frontiers and hypervolume. The results show that learning the location of the phases together with their values provides competitive or improved fidelity--resource trade-offs, although the advantage depends on the target state and qudit dimension.

Abstract: Robust quantum control usually assumes one of two extremes: the error is of a known form and only its strength is unknown, or nothing at all is known about it. In practice one often sits in between. The size of a static error is unknown, but its class is known, for example, it only shifts energy levels, or it acts on each subsystem separately. We ask how fast a control can cancel, to first order, every error in such a class. A time-independent Hamiltonian can do this if and only if all errors in the class have zero diagonal in a common basis. In that case an equally spaced energy ladder in this basis does it after one period. A lower bound that holds for any time-dependent control with bounded drive power shows that this simple constant control is optimal for several natural classes, including unknown level shifts and unknown local errors on two subsystems. Completely unknown errors need a time-dependent control, and there, knowing the error class shortens the minimum time by a factor $\sqrt{d^2+1}$. So partial knowledge of the error changes not only how long an optimal robust control takes, but also its form.

Abstract: Reliable characterization of quantum states and quantum processes is central to the development of scalable quantum computing. Standard characterization techniques such as full quantum state tomography become rapidly impractical as system size increases. In this work we present two software-oriented fidelity estimation protocols that avoid full tomographic reconstruction while remaining experimentally relevant for noisy intermediate-scale quantum devices. The first protocol is direct fidelity estimation (DFE), where the fidelity between a target state and an experimentally prepared state is estimated from importance-sampled Pauli measurements. The second protocol is a survival-probability-based method that reconstructs fidelity from experimentally accessible moments of a noisy evolution and its approximate inverse, and is intrinsically robust against state-preparation-and-measurement (SPAM) errors. We discuss the theoretical basis of both approaches, analyze their noise sensitivity, and present numerical evidence on representative multiqubit states under depolarizing, dephasing, and amplitude-damping channels. For DFE we show that attenuation-aware correction improves robustness against measurement noise. For the survival-probability approach we show that an $L_2$-optimized expansion substantially outperforms naive Taylor truncation in high-noise regimes.

By determining the leading exponential scaling of switching rates between metastable attractors, we predict the stationary magnetization, the first-order dissipative phase-transition point, and the exponential scaling of slow relaxation rates. These predictions agree with quantum master-equation calculations and an exact analytical result for the second model without dephasing. We also examine how the choice of quasiprobability representation affects numerical convergence and find that the alternative semiclassical Wigner approach, based on Gaussian truncation, has varying accuracy across models and parameter regimes. These results substantiate the framework's applicability to experimentally relevant collective spin systems with both collective and local dissipation.

Abstract: High-order harmonic generation from molecules is highly sensitive to molecular geometry and strong-field electron dynamics, providing a powerful route for probing and controlling ultrafast processes in extended systems. These findings demonstrate that molecular-size engineering offers an effective strategy for controlling harmonic emission and optimizing attosecond light sources in extended molecular systems.

Abstract: Distributing entanglement directly between error-corrected logical qubits is an important capability for scalable quantum networks. The protocol combines motional-control and photonic-networking operations that have already been demonstrated in trapped-ion experiments.

Abstract: Finite rate randomness expansion (RE) which is secure against quantum adversaries is a crucial information processing task which is well studied in the case of two non-communicating parties sharing Bell non-local correlations in a device independent manner. We propose a template SDI-RE protocol which is applicable for arbitrary $N$-cycle non-contextuality (NC) inequalities and derive its RE rate under finite statistics. As a special case, we also highlight the advantages of the situation when RE is constrained by Clauser-Horne-Shimony-Holt (CHSH) inequality but with characterized measurements implemented by only a single party. Our template protocol also incorporates non-ideal statistics which may not satisfy perfect commutativity and perfect repeatability, both of which are necessary in a loophole-free test of NC inequalities. As such, several recent experimental implementations of significantly loophole-free tests of NC inequalities can be modified for secure RE following our approach.

Method: 理论分析方面，利用Bloch球面的几何性质证明串行电路存在"相位锁定"现象，推导其切向方向数上界(2E+2)与傅里叶系数需求(2E+1)的关系，并与双量子比特控制电路(至少6E+6个方向)对比。实验方面，在平谱目标的精确损失函数和三个真实世界时间序列数据集上，使用梯度下降、Adam和Levenberg-Marquardt三种优化器，进行100次独立运行，统计在固定步数预算内达到最优解的成功率，以及测试集误差表现。

Abstract: Selected configuration interaction (SCI) obtains accurate many-body solutions by variationally diagonalising the Hamiltonian in a sparse, iteratively selected set of configurations, but can lose compactness when the target state has broad configurational support. We introduce selected stabilizer interaction (SSI), a fully classical, SCI-inspired method that instead iteratively constructs a nonorthogonal subspace spanned by a systematically enlargeable set of selected stabilizer states. Motivation: 高精度时间估计在量子计量学中具有重要应用价值。虽然双光子关联量子态和N00N态在精密测量中展现出潜力,但如何在实际实验中最大化利用量子资源、达到量子Fisher信息给出的理论精度极限,以及如何处理系统误差和损耗对测量精度的影响,仍需深入研究。传统的双探测器方案可能无法充分提取量子态的全部信息,因此需要探索更优的测量方案来提升时间延迟估计的精度。

Abstract: In this work we study the application of two-photon correlated quantum states for high-precision time estimation. To achieve this, photon pairs were fed into a Hong-Ou-Mandel polarization interferometer to generate N00N entangled states with $N = 2$. Two- and four-detector schemes were considered, and the achievable time estimation precision of each was studied, with particular emphasis on the system's Fisher information in each case. Our results reveal that using a four-detector scheme, enabling the measurement of both coincidence and photon-bunching terms, achieves the maximum precision given by the quantum Fisher information, without the need of characterizing system losses. We also show that correlations between repetitions arise due to systematic errors, limiting the precision enhancement that can be obtained by increasing the number of repetitions. Taking these correlations into account, we show that the four-detector scheme outperforms the two-detector scheme in the precision of time-delay estimation. With the experimentally determined calibration uncertainties, the four-detector scheme improves the dispersion by a factor of 2 for the repetitions performed, reaching up to a factor of 4 as both schemes approach their systematic floors.

Abstract: We examine how a finite many-body system can function as an effective charger for a single-qubit quantum battery, using the Lipkin-Meshkov-Glick model as a paradigmatic setting. As our figure of merit, we consider the battery ergotropy while explicitly resolving the energetic flows across the composite system. In the absence of intra-charger interactions, we show that the generation of non-zero ergotropy is controlled by the competition between collective coupling and spectral mismatch. We further demonstrate that even passive positive-temperature charger states can generate finite battery ergotropy, provided the charger is sufficiently large and sufficiently hot. Intra-charger interactions restructure the charger spectrum and qualitatively modify the available energetic pathways. While they can enhance the amount of energy accessible for charging, they can simultaneously divert energy into internal many-body and battery-charger interaction channels, producing an energetic impedance to direct charger-to-battery transfer. Consequently, battery ergotropy alone is insufficient to characterize an effective many-body charger: the useful work generated and its energetic provenance must be assessed together.

Abstract: Classical simulation of quantum many-body dynamics is challenged by the rapid growth of operator complexity, particularly in higher dimensions where tensor-network methods become increasingly demanding. We develop state-adapted Pauli-weight propagation based on generalized $m$-body mean-field projections, which compress many-body observables into optimal low-body representations in a covariance geometry induced by the initial state. Combined with adaptive Krylov-like restricted Heisenberg dynamics, this provides an alternative to conventional Hilbert--Schmidt Pauli-weight truncation. We derive necessary and conditional sufficient bounds on low-body approximation accuracy using standard operator complexity measures and characterize possible dynamical failure mechanisms of the state-adapted method. Numerical benchmarks demonstrate substantial state-dependent accuracy gains, while identifying regimes of reduced accuracy or violations of Pauli spectral bounds. For a three-dimensional system of $80$ interacting spins, our method achieves strong body-order convergence, close agreement with independently converging tensor-network simulations, and substantially smaller sparse Pauli representations. These results establish state-adapted Pauli propagation as a promising approach to classical simulation of higher-dimensional coherent many-body dynamics.

Abstract: We introduce patchwork, a lattice-surgery compiler for arbitrary multi-qubit Pauli measurements on a prescribed narrow-corridor two-dimensional surface-code layout. We introduce measurement circuits and schedules that, together with protruding lobes of $Y$-extension patches, preserve the full effective code distance for all numerically tested code distances. To support parallel decoding, we develop a wavefront algorithm that partitions merged lattice-surgery patches into space-time commit, buffer, and cleanup regions. Simulations of three sequential multi-qubit Pauli measurements acting on ten logical qubits show that staged decoding achieves total failure probabilities close to those of monolithic decoding over the parameters tested. These methods provide an automated framework for fault-tolerant lattice surgery and a route toward scalable, real-time decoding.

Abstract: Greenberger-Horne-Zeilinger (GHZ) states are multipartite entangled quantum states that play a central role in quantum information theory, with applications in quantum communication, cryptography, and distributed quantum computing. A major experimental goal is to create high-dimensional GHZ states using multiple entangled parties. Krenn, Gu and Zeilinger discovered a surprising bridge between these quantum-optical experiments and graph theory: certain photonic experiments can be represented by edge-coloured, edge-weighted graphs, and the dimension of the produced GHZ state equals the number of feasible monochromatic inherited vertex colourings. This led Krenn and Gu to conjecture that every GHZ graph with more than four vertices has dimension at most $2$. A counterexample would give a new high-dimensional multipartite GHZ construction, while a proof would clarify fundamental resource limitations in quantum optics. It is known that the conjecture holds for graphs of vertex connectivity at most $2$ [MFCS 2024], and that simple GHZ graphs with $n>4$ vertices have dimension less than $n/\sqrt{2}$ [Quantum 2024]. However, no general upper bound depending only on vertex connectivity was known. We prove that every simple connected GHZ graph $G$ with at least four vertices has dimension at most $2^{κ(G)-1}+\binom{κ(G)}{2}$, where $κ(G)$ is the vertex connectivity of $G$. We also prove new bounds in terms of edge connectivity and maximum degree.

Abstract: Semiconductor spin qubits offer a promising route to compact and scalable quantum computers, combining long coherence times in isotopically purified silicon with compatibility with semiconductor manufacturing. We develop a detailed energy budget, identifying cryogenic cooling as the dominant baseline contribution before quantum error correction (QEC). The comparison between the QFT and the classical FFT reveals that, for any quantum workload operating under a polynomially bounded sampling regime, the crossover in energy consumption occurs at smaller problem sizes than the crossover in execution time. Thus, quantum computing can become energetically competitive before achieving a speedup in execution time. We then incorporate surface-code QEC and analyze the energy costs of QEC cycles, gate synthesis, and decoding. At scale, instrumentation and decoding become dominant contributions, while cryogenic cooling imposes a constraint on scalability. We show that optimizing the control-signal demultiplexing ratio can reduce the overall energy cost by approximately two orders of magnitude. For a system with about one million physical qubits, we estimate a power consumption of approximately 100 kW, at least one order of magnitude below that of today's highest-performance classical computing systems.

Whether the long-time dynamics is dominated by scars is determined by the real parts of the eigenvalues, which are set by the ratio between the two relaxation rates $γ_{\pm}$ describing spin flips down-to-up or up-to-down respectively. We evaluate SynDebug across multiple QEC code families, code distances, and encoded workloads, including state preparation, entanglement validation, and Shor-style programs. Our results demonstrate that SynDebug enables practical FTQC debugging with an average 1.8% additional circuit depth overhead (0% in most cases) and no measurable increase in the logical error rate, showing that systems-level debugging for large-scale FTQC applications is both feasible and practical.

Abstract: The classical capacity of a noisy optical channel is the maximum rate at which information can be transmitted reliably, given a constraint on the mean input energy. For the ubiquitous bosonic Gaussian channels, which include standard models of loss and noise in fibre and free-space optical links, determining this rate requires addressing whether entanglement between channel uses or non-Gaussian signals can outperform Gaussian encodings. More precisely, the question is whether the energy-constrained Holevo information is additive across arbitrarily many channel uses and coincides with its Gaussian counterpart. We answer these fundamental questions in the affirmative for every single-mode bosonic Gaussian channel at every input energy. Namely, we prove that independently prepared Gaussian states with Gaussian-distributed random displacements achieve the capacity, including in the low-energy regime where squeezing the signals competes with encoding information in their displacement. Our proof connects an entanglement inequality to a sharp, additive lower bound on output entropy. We then characterise communication above capacity: under a strict total photon-number cutoff, the probability of correct decoding decreases exponentially with the number of channel uses, and we derive explicit bounds on the decay exponent. Together, these results identify the ultimate limits of classical communication for some of the most fundamental models of optical communication.

Abstract: The quantum-trajectory description of a noisy quantum circuit is not unique: every Kraus representation of the noise channel defines a different unraveling, i.e., a different ensemble of pure states that averages to the same density matrix. While all unravelings share the same expectation values of observables, they generally differ in the amount of quantum resources carried by the individual trajectories. Here we study non-stabilizerness, or magic, quantified by the stabilizer Rényi entropy, in quantum-trajectory unravelings of Haar-random and $T$-doped random Clifford brickwork circuits subject to local dephasing noise of strength $p$. The canonical unraveling in terms of Pauli jumps is Clifford and leaves magic untouched, so that trajectories retain the Haar-random value of non-stabilizerness. Exploring the full two-parameter family of minimal unravelings, we find that the trajectory-averaged magic can be substantially reduced by a state-independent choice of Kraus operators, and that the optimal choice is not in general the projective-measurement unraveling, but a two-outcome generalized measurement in which the Kraus operators correspond to a weak measurement of the noisy qubit. The optimal unraveling reduces the extensive magic density of the trajectories by a $p$-dependent factor without altering its linear scaling with system size. At the same time, it produces a very broad distribution of magic over trajectories, with a sizable fraction of trajectories retaining Haar-random magic. Since the cost of stabilizer-based classical simulation grows exponentially with the magic of the state, these high-magic trajectories, rather than the average, control the simulation cost. Our results delineate the potential and the limitations of unraveling optimization as a route to the classical simulation of noisy quantum dynamics with stabilizer methods.

</details>

Abstract: Quantum low-density parity-check (qLDPC) codes have a canonical mixed state---the normalized projector onto the code space---that lies at the intersection of quantum error correction and quantum many-body physics, yet the stability of its information-theoretic structure under local noise remains poorly understood. Recent progress on mixed-state phases of matter has identified the spatial decay of conditional mutual information (CMI) $I(A:C|B)$ as a key diagnostic of such stability, analogous to the role of the spectral gap for quantum ground states. At strong noise, exponential decay holds whenever the buffer supports no logical operator, since correlations can persist only along rare chains of buffer qudits that remain informative. Thus, the exponential decay of the noise-induced CMI provides a general criterion for mixed-state phase stability.

Abstract: Significant efforts are currently being directed toward the implementation of large-scale quantum information processors, but realizing useful quantum advantage also requires the development of efficient quantum algorithms. Abstract: In analytically tractable models of Einstein-scalar critical collapse, it has been shown that the quantum self-energy of the collapsing matter in the dominant $s$-wave sector grows with Lyapunov exponent $D-2$, eventually competing with the classical instability that controls black hole formation. In CSS, the classical approach to a naked singularity is interrupted by the appearance of trapped surfaces. At large $D$, the horizon tracing reduces to a Bessel problem, yielding a quantum wall whose radius obeys $r_h\propto(D-2)^{-2}$. This radius can remain above the Planck scale at finite $D$, although maintaining this hierarchy becomes increasingly demanding as $D$ grows. In DSS, the same growth is modulated by echoing and can retain memory of previous echoes. The simplest anomaly-induced action exhibits accumulating memory but is singular at the spherical center. We construct a covariant one-loop action whose stress tensor remains smooth throughout the DSS domain and organize it into an echo-periodic structure without memory. This suggests that the smooth vacuum polarization of the genuine four-dimensional DSS Choptuik spacetime may similarly be governed by its exact echoing symmetry.

Abstract: Near the threshold between scattering and capture by a Schwarzschild black hole, a particle spends a long time close to an unstable circular orbit, which in the ultrarelativistic limit approaches the light ring. Here we study the behavior of the gravitational wave fluxes emitted by such an ultrarelativistic particle at first order in the mass ratio. The fluxes radiated to infinity and absorbed by the horizon diverge as the inverse distance from the light ring multiplied by its logarithm. Using the Wentzel, Kramers, and Brillouin (WKB) approximation to solve the Regge-Wheeler equation and treating the infinite harmonic sum analytically, we derive an exact expression for the coefficient of this logarithmic enhancement and show that it is identical for both fluxes. We then estimate the coefficients of the subleading pole contributions by combining numerical calculations at low multipoles with the exact asymptotics at large multipoles. We further conjecture an extension to equatorial circular orbits in Kerr. Interestingly, our results also inform the analytical structure of the energy and angular momentum losses of ultrarelativistic scattering orbits near the light ring. More broadly, our work illustrates how WKB methods can provide the asymptotic behavior of self-force observables in regimes where numerical mode sums become increasingly challenging.

Abstract: Current pulsar timing arrays could detect gravitational wave memory, which would provide a unique test of general relativity. We additionally test a noise marginalized version of this statistic, and compare it's effectiveness to the simpler maximum posterior method. We perform these tests on simulated pulsar timing array with red and white noise content, as well as the NANOGrav $15$-year data set. We find no significant variations in our detection statistics between the noise-marginalized and the maximum-posterior methods for the simulated datasets, and only small variations in the NANOGrav $15$-year comparisons. We conclude that for realistic PTAs the noise-marginalization method could be useful for assigning a false alarm probability to Bayesian results, though the maximum posterior method is sufficient for identifying potentially detectable signals.

Abstract: This thesis maps two exotic black-hole geometries onto the transonic flow of a de Laval nozzle and reads their perturbative response in aerodynamic variables. The two corrections are distinguished by which channel responds and by how strongly all of them do, the quantum coupling moving the quality factor by up to 37% in the scalar and Dirac channels and by about 10% in the other two, while the hair acts on every channel alike. Identifying the two tortoise coordinates turns the equality of the potentials into a Sturm-Liouville problem, so that the prescribed barrier fixes the wall profile and with it the flow variables and the thrust coefficient. The hair is felt across the whole divergent section, whereas the quantum correction registers only near the throat and grows as the inverse fourth power of the mass, which confines it to primordial black holes. The analogy is kinematical, so the duct reproduces the scattering of perturbations off these geometries without reproducing the field equations behind them.

The shot-noise-limited sensitivity floor is $S^{1/2}_n=1.6\times10^{-20}\,\mathrm{Hz}^{-1/2}$. Abstract: We study the bound states perturbations in the interior of the Schwarzschild black hole and examine whether or not their microstates spectrum can furnish a statistical description of the black hole entropy. These states contain a discrete set of modes characterized by the labels $(s, p,\ell,m)$, where $s$ represents the spin of the species, $p$ denotes the radial excitation number while $(\ell,m)$ are the usual angular momentum labels. Requiring that the shortest wavelength resolved by the highly excited bound states remains above the quantum gravity scale introduces an angular cutoff $\ell_{\rm max}\sim r_H/L_{\rm Pl}$. The resulting angular degeneracy therefore scales with the horizon area. We count the corresponding configurations in the microcanonical ensemble and find an entropy of the form $S_{\rm bs}=αA/\hbar G$. Demanding that the bound state sector to be in thermal equilibrium with the black hole at the Hawking temperature fixes the fraction of the bound state energy in total black hole energy. For the gravitational perturbations sector, the equilibrium bound state contribution in entropy and the energy fraction are typically small. We also examine the conditions under which the bound states could account for the full black hole entropy. Although such saturation is possible at the level of state counting, it requires an order unity fraction in the black hole energy and therefore the backreactions on the background geometry cannot be ignored.

</details>

Abstract: We investigate the fate of big-bang singularity and coherence between distinct clock states in a quantum cosmological setting within the Page-Wootters framework. With a canonically quantized plane-symmetric Bianchi I universe, we employ Dirac factorization of the Wheeler-DeWitt constraint and obtain a Schrodinger-like equation which is first-order in an internal clock variable. By constructing its solution as a Gaussian wave packet in the expanding branch, we derive the corresponding conditional state and probability density of the universe. We find that the conditional probability density vanishes in the zero-volume limit, indicating resolution of the big-bang singularity within this probabilistic framework. Another prespective of the study is provided by the construction of density matrix from the conditional states associated with different clock readings and the remaining degree of freedom. By tracing out the remaining subsystem, we obtain the reduced density matrix and analyze the resulting coherence as a function of separation between different clock states. These results provide a relational characterization of singularity resolution and demonstrate how the spectral properties of the quantum state govern the temporal coherence between different clock states in the Page-Wootters description.

Abstract: A confident detection of orbital eccentricity in gravitational-wave signals from black hole-neutron star systems would be a strong indicator of their dynamical formation origin. While eccentric waveform models have recently been developed, their validity for analyzing black hole--neutron star signals has yet to be tested against numerical-relativity simulations. Building upon our previous study of quasicircular black hole--neutron star mergers, we perform simulations of a subset of these systems with orbital eccentricities around five times larger than those inferred for GW200105_162426. Additionally, we perform simulations with magnetized matter to assess whether trapped $g$-mode oscillations persist in strongly magnetized disks. Regarding the emitted gravitational-wave signal, we find that the effective one-body waveform model SEOBNRv5EHM shows reasonable agreement unless tidal contributions become important, and the inspiral-only post-Newtonian models pyEFPE/pyEFPEHM show larger disagreement in the late inspiral. For the equal-mass system, which has the largest effective tidal deformability, eccentric tidal corrections have a small but noticeable impact on the phase, $\sim0.1$ rad. We find an almost negligible impact of eccentricity on the properties of the remnant black hole, and differences of up to a factor of two in the remnant disk mass. Trapped $g$ modes in the accretion disks have the same frequency regardless of the eccentricity, confirming that they depend mainly on the properties of the remnant black hole. We find that when accretion disks are strongly magnetized, they become less dense and have less pronounced structure, and that the trapped $g$ modes previously identified in the unmagnetized cases are efficiently damped.

Abstract: We investigate primordial black hole (PBH) formation from primordial magnetic fields generated during a non-singular ekpyrotic bounce. A time-dependent coupling between the electromagnetic field and a scalar background breaks conformal invariance and amplifies gauge field fluctuations during contraction, generating magnetic fields whose anisotropic stress dominates during radiation domination. By retaining finite-wavenumber cutoffs, we compute the ultraviolet-dominated curvature spectrum, evaluate density-perturbation variance, and determine PBH abundances using both monochromatic and critical-collapse prescriptions. For benchmark parameters, the critical collapse mass spectrum peaks at $M\simeq 2.0\times10^{-3}M_\odot$ , with a total PBH dark matter fraction of $f_{PBH}\approx 10^{-5}$, forming a subdominant subsolar PBH population. Enforcing the cosmological overclosure bound ($f_{\mathrm{PBH}} \le 1$) constrains the primary background parameters, namely the end-of-contraction Hubble scale ($H_{\mathrm{end}} \le 1.6 \times 10^3~\mathrm{GeV}$) and the contraction exponent ($q \le 0.247$), establishing a physical boundary consistent with the electromagnetic backreaction limit. Shifting to asteroid-mass PBHs or a larger dark matter fraction requires modifying the ultraviolet cutoff and contraction duration. This analysis highlights the essential role of finite-wavenumber cutoffs and assumed-collapse prescriptions in linking bounce magnetogenesis to the predicted PBH abundance.

Abstract: We construct an exact Simpson-Visser solution in metric bumblebee gravity, supported by a phantom scalar, magnetic nonlinear electrodynamics, and a spacelike configuration of fixed norm. The independent vector equation determines a local Lagrange multiplier and a temporal component that permits a regular ingoing extension across metric horizons. Reconstruction of the matter functions identifies a continuous family of wormholes, null minimum geometries, and black-bounces within one action. In the black hole case, the areal photon sphere and critical timelike orbit radii retain their Schwarzschild values, whereas angular deflection, periapsis advance, and photon orbit instability depend on the bounce and bumblebee parameters. The metric shadow coincides with the Schwarzschild prediction at fixed lapse mass and areal observer position; in addition, the adopted Sgr A* shadow diameter and M87* angular mass comparisons do not determine these parameters separately. The coupled axial equations yield distinct tensor, metric, and transverse bumblebee characteristics. We compute fundamental auxiliary massless scalar and ordinary Maxwell probe resonances by Chebyshev spectral collocation and estimate the tensor and bumblebee resonance scales at leading geometric optics order. Nonnegative probe potentials exclude exponentially growing probe modes. For negative nonminimal coupling and a nonzero temporal case, however, the supporting electromagnetic field develops an angular gradient instability in its extraordinary polarization. The unstable characteristic persists in the coupled principal symbol, while a variational construction establishes growing modes on the fixed geometry and lower bounds on their growth rates.

For equatorial prograde returns we find, with $κ=\sqrt{1-a^2/M^2}$ and $X=nκ$, $ε_n=\sqrt{3}\,κ/(e^{2πX}-1) +O(κ^2|\lnκ|)$. This single law connects the extremal inverse-winding hierarchy to subextremal exponential accumulation. Perturbing the return family quasi-equatorially, we derive a second scaling law for the polar Jacobi phase, $Ψ_θ=2\ln[\sinh(πX)/κ]+C_s+o(1)$, and show that its focusing points cross from geometric spacing in winding number to asymptotically uniform spacing $Δ\mathcal N\simeq(2κ)^{-1}$. Numerical integrations of the exact Kerr radial dynamics and linear polar equation verify both scaling collapses. Thus near extremal Kerr exhibits parametrically distinct winding and polar-focusing scales, rather than a discontinuous jump between extremal and subextremal lensing hierarchies.

Abstract: Hydrodynamic modeling of viscous neutron stars requires a choice of theory of relativistic hydrodynamics. That distinct formulations contain the same physical content in the long-wavelength limit is referred to as ``hydrodynamic universality.'' It has been recently shown that hydrodynamic universality can break down in radial neutron star oscillations: causality and finite-size effects restrict access to the long-wavelength universal hydrodynamic regime in sufficiently viscous stars. We show that this breakdown occurs for a broad range of causal hydrodynamic frames in Bemfica--Disconzi--Noronha--Kovtun (BDNK) theory. Increasing viscosity collectively pushes this class of causal frames away from the BDNK regime of validity, eventually forcing all to lie outside it at bulk viscosities in the range $ζ\sim10^{30}$--$10^{31}\mathrm{g}/\mathrm{cm}/\mathrm{s}$. This breakdown of universality is accompanied by material changes in the oscillation mode structure: non-hydrodynamic modes become damped on timescales comparable to those of hydrodynamic modes, the first overtone can become longer lived than the fundamental mode, and unphysical instabilities with sub-millisecond timescales can emerge. Even in regimes where universality has broken down, we find that the inviscid threshold of gravitational collapse remains unchanged in BDNK theory. These results provide evidence that first-order theories of relativistic hydrodynamics may be insufficient for modeling sufficiently viscous neutron stars.

</details>

Abstract: We obtain an explicit separation of the coupled gravitational and electromagnetic radiative equations on nonextremal Kerr--Newman--(anti-)de Sitter black holes with nonzero charge and rotation. The construction uses a matrix with constant determinant, chosen independently of the frequency and azimuthal number, and treats the positive- and negative-spin sectors within the same formulation. We establish that this transformation is smooth and invertible throughout the open AdS exterior and between the event and cosmological horizons in dS. The resulting radial and angular systems are valid at arbitrary complex frequencies. We also express regularity of the radiative Newman--Penrose variables at the rotation axes and future horizons as conditions on the separated solutions. In the asymptotically flat limit, the transformation is related to Hintz's matrix integrating factor by the allowed frame freedom. These results provide a basis for studying quasinormal modes and black-hole stability in Kerr--Newman--(anti-)de Sitter spacetimes, as well as exploring holographic applications to strongly coupled systems.

Abstract: The RNS formalism describing string theory on $\mathrm{AdS}_3 \times \mathbf{S}^3 \times \mathrm{K3}$ supported by pure NS-NS flux is in principle straightforward to write down. One can directly check that the worldsheet theory reproduces the correct chiral spectrum expected from supergravity by analyzing the BRST cohomology. However, when one attempts to construct the worldsheet partition function using the standard rules of the RNS formalism by appropriately summing over spin structures to ensure worldsheet modular invariance, one finds a result in tension with spacetime supersymmetry. We explain why this is the case and, more importantly, propose a resolution. Specifically, we fix the physical state projection for the partition function uniquely by requiring spacetime supersymmetry. Perhaps surprisingly, the projected worldsheet partition function does not factorize into a bosonic and a fermionic part and involves functions that are not modular covariant. As a consistency check, we show that our prescription for the worldsheet partition function reproduces the supergravity spectrum for both Type IIB and IIA theories. Furthermore, we compute the elliptic genus of the dual CFT from the worldsheet, which has both a holomorphic short string and a non-holomorphic long string contribution. The latter displays characteristics of a mock modular function, albeit with some key differences, while the former has the tantalizing structure of a symmetric orbifold. We also point out the worldsheet construction of a non-supersymmetric theory, whose spectrum is free of tachyons.

</details>

Abstract: We derive a large-N wormhole saddle in the Sachdev-Ye-Kitaev (SYK) model for the variance of the thermal one-point function of a heavy operator across realizations of the microscopic couplings. We propose that this saddle describes a double trumpet geometry. In the double-scaling limit, we express the wormhole as a transition amplitude in an emergent bulk Hilbert space of chords connecting the two boundaries. In the Schwarzian limit, the chords proliferate and the wormhole defines a quantum transition amplitude in the open-universe Hilbert space of Jackiw-Teitelboim (JT) gravity. Comparing the SYK wormhole amplitude with its counterpart in JT gravity coupled to matter provides a direct test of the heavy-operator randomness hypothesis in SYK.
  The microscopic SYK couplings specify the α-parameters of the wormhole. For a fixed realization of the couplings, we compute the observable in the corresponding α-state. We provide the bulk wavefunction of the associated half-wormhole, which exhibits erratic correlations in the length basis that do not self-average. We extend the bulk description to the Hilbert space of a closed universe. We point out that the correlation chords from which the wormhole geometry emerges structurally resemble local observer worldlines.

</details>


### [91] [Region bounds, error margins and certified positivity in the numerical conformal bootstrap; Or the first mathematically rigorous bounds on the 3d Ising CFT critical exponents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20701)

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 数值共形自举界通常被称为"严格"，但实际上存在四个关键缺陷使其无法成为真正的数学定理：(1)仅在采样点而非参数空间区域上成立；(2)使用截断的有理近似而非真实共形块；(3)仅考虑有限自旋列表；(4)存在浮点舍入误差。这些缺陷限制了自举方法的数学严格性，需要系统性解决方案将数值结果转化为可证明的定理。

Method: 提出四项核心技术方法：(1)开发在参数空间区域(包括外部维度空间)上高效搜索正泛函的算法；(2)将大自旋交叉矢量的正性作为区域约束条件处理；(3)建立共形块递归关系的新解析界，确保泛函在真实未截断交叉矢量上为正；(4)构建精确算术认证系统，包括三变量Bernstein基正性证书、Q(√2)上的精确算术、无穷自旋尾部的紧化处理，以及真实块与截断之间差异的显式界，将无穷多个正性条件简化为有限个机器可验证的符号条件。

Result: 成功认证了(Δ_σ, Δ_ε)平面上的排除区域：对于Δ_σ∈[0.505, 0.5405]，除包含3d Ising岛的小矩形外，整个Δ_ε∈(1/2, 3]区域被排除。在明确的谱假设下，获得临界指数η和ν的严格双边界。证明了混合σ-ε系统的解析输入，这将移除自旋2能隙假设。区域界方法将岛屿映射简化为少量一维二分搜索，提高了自举搜索效率并防止了常见失败模式。所有核心代码和机器可检查证书已公开发布。

Conclusion: 本研究成功填补了数值共形自举与严格数学定理之间的所有关键缺口，建立了完整的认证框架。通过精确算术和解析界的结合，首次将自举排除界提升为可机器验证的数学定理。对3d Ising模型的应用展示了方法的实用性，为共形场论研究提供了严格的数学基础。区域搜索技术不仅增强了严格性，还显著提升了计算效率，为未来自举研究设立了新的标准。

Abstract: Numerical conformal bootstrap bounds are commonly called rigorous, yet as published they are not theorems: they hold at sampled points rather than on regions of parameter space, for truncated rational approximations of the conformal blocks, for a finite list of spins, and up to floating-point rounding. We close all four gaps. We show how to search efficiently for functionals positive on regions, including external-dimension space; how positivity on the large-spin crossing vectors can be imposed as such a region constraint; how new analytic bounds on the conformal-block recursion relations give functionals positive on the true, untruncated crossing vectors; and how to certify the positivity of these functionals in exact arithmetic. The certification rests on Bernstein-basis positivity certificates in up to three variables, exact arithmetic in $\mathbb{Q}(\sqrt2)$, a compactified treatment of the infinite spin tail, and an explicit bound on the difference between the true blocks and their truncations, so that infinitely many positivity conditions reduce to finitely many machine-checked sign conditions. As a flagship application we certify an exclusion region in the $(Δ_σ,Δ_ε)$ plane: for $Δ_σ\in[0.505,0.5405]$ all of $Δ_ε\in(1/2,3]$ is excluded except a small rectangle containing the 3d Ising island, giving rigorous two-sided bounds on the critical exponents $η$ and $ν$ under explicitly stated spectrum assumptions. The analytic inputs for the mixed $σ$-$ε$ system, which would remove the spin-2 gap assumption, are also proved. The same methods prevent common practical failure modes and can make bootstrap searches faster: region bounds turn the mapping of an island into a handful of one-dimensional bisections. The essential code to reproduce the search and the certification, and all machine-checkable certificates, are released.

</details>

Abstract: We develop a finite-cutoff holographic description of the double-scaled SYK (DSSYK) model. We construct microscopic deformations of Kourkoulou-Maldacena and thermofield-double SYK states whose double-scaling limits reproduce a lower-dimensional analogue of $\text{T}\overline{\text{T}}$ and $\text{T}\overline{\text{T}}+Λ_2$ deformations, which we denote $T^2$ and $T^2+Λ_1$, respectively. The ensemble-averaged theory describes wormhole states in finite-cutoff sine dilaton gravity, and finite-cutoff anti-de Sitter and de Sitter geometries in the low- and high-energy regimes, respectively. The analysis includes both perturbative and non-perturbative solutions to the energy spectrum flow equation, and the regions behind and beyond the respective horizons. Only the perturbative branch admits physical quantum states. We study how to incorporate end-of-the-world (ETW) branes in the finite cutoff holographic dictionary. The ETW brane leads to an additional boundary theory deformation, which leads to black hole or eternal traversable wormhole geometries depending on the existence of normalizable bound states. We extend the finite cutoff length evaluations for constant mean curvature (CMC) slices, and interpret them as complexity in the boundary theory. We discuss possible extensions with matter. Our results provide a microscopic framework for studying the finite-cutoff boundaries, CMC slices and branes in the holographic dictionary of DSSYK.

</details>

Method: 1. 在四维de Sitter空间的膨胀Poincaré patch中建立观察者中心的S矩阵形式体系；2. 沿共动（永恒）观察者的世界线定义渐近态，这是唯一在该patch中对所有固有时都存在的测地观察者；3. 以观察者固有时平移的守恒荷作为自由哈密顿量；4. 证明构造的S矩阵独立于具体选择哪个永恒观察者；5. 将该框架应用于库仑规范下的纯Yang-Mills理论；6. 在树级水平计算三胶子和四胶子散射振幅；7. 分析振幅的结构特征及其与de Sitter等距Ward恒等式的关系。

Result: 成功构建了观察者独立的de Sitter时空S矩阵框架。在库仑规范纯Yang-Mills理论中，树级三胶子和四胶子振幅被明确计算出来。这些振幅展现出与宇宙学中规范流关联函数相似的数学结构。振幅严格满足de Sitter等距群的Ward恒等式约束，体现了时空对称性对散射过程的限制。该结果为在膨胀宇宙背景下研究量子场论散射过程提供了具体可行的计算框架。

Conclusion: 本文成功建立了de Sitter空间中观察者中心的S矩阵理论框架，解决了膨胀时空中散射振幅定义的根本困难。通过沿永恒观察者世界线定义渐近态和使用固有时演化的哈密顿量，构造出与观察者选择无关的S矩阵。在Yang-Mills理论的具体应用中，计算的胶子散射振幅不仅结构上类似宇宙学关联函数，还受de Sitter对称性的Ward恒等式严格约束，验证了该框架的自洽性。这为在加速膨胀宇宙背景下发展量子场论和研究早期宇宙的粒子物理过程提供了重要的理论工具。

Abstract: We construct an observer-centric S-matrix in the expanding Poincaré patch of four-dimensional de Sitter space. The asymptotic states are defined along the worldline of a comoving, or eternal, observer, the only kind of geodesic observer that remains in the patch for all proper time, and the free Hamiltonian is the conserved charge generating translations in her proper time. The resulting S-matrix is independent of which eternal observer is chosen. We apply the construction to pure Yang--Mills theory in Coulomb gauge and compute the three- and four-gluon amplitudes at tree level. The amplitudes have a structure similar to the cosmological correlators of gauge currents and are constrained by the Ward identities of de Sitter isometries.

These results establish how the relative orientation between a Lorentz-violating background and an anisotropic compactification lattice enters the renormalized vacuum energy and its directional response.

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 理解量子系统热化过程中非稳定性(nonstabilizerness)和"魔力"(magic)的演化规律是量子信息和量子计算领域的重要问题。虽然纠缠熵在热化中的作用已被广泛研究,但非稳定性资源在热化动力学中的行为及其普适性尚未明确。特别是"魔力屏障"现象——即在纠缠快速增长时魔力出现瞬态峰值——是否是量子遍历系统的普遍特征需要理论验证。本研究旨在通过随机矩阵理论(RMT)框架系统地分析这一现象,揭示其与量子混沌和热化的本质联系。

Method: 研究采用随机矩阵理论方法,从纯积态出发,分析由三种高斯系综(正交、酉、辛系综)随机矩阵哈密顿量生成的酉演化动力学。具体方法包括:1)解析计算纠缠熵、反平坦度(antiflatness)、纠缠容量(capacity of entanglement)和非局域迹距离魔力(nonlocal trace distance magic)的时间演化;2)研究这些量子信息量在热化过程中的动力学行为,特别是瞬态最大值的出现时间;3)将不同非稳定性度量的特征时间尺度与纠缠增长速率的最大值时间进行比较;4)通过随机矩阵理论的普适性论证魔力屏障在量子混沌系统中的普遍存在性。

Result: 研究发现:1)反平坦度、纠缠容量和非局域魔力的瞬态最大值出现在与纠缠熵最大增长率相同的特征时间尺度上;2)这一"魔力屏障"现象在所有三种高斯随机矩阵系综中都普遍存在;3)魔力屏障的出现与随机矩阵的谱性质密切相关;4)该现象在随机矩阵理论描述的热化过程中具有普适性,表明其可能是所有量子混沌系统的共同特征;5)不同的非稳定性度量(反平坦度、纠缠容量、非局域魔力)在动力学演化中表现出时间尺度上的一致性。

Conclusion: 魔力屏障是随机矩阵理论热化过程的普遍特征,其出现的特征时间尺度与纠缠快速增长的时间尺度一致,这表明该现象在量子混沌系统中具有普适性。由于非稳定性资源在热化早期阶段表现出显著的瞬态增长,遍历量子理论热化过程的定量模拟本质上需要量子计算才能有效实现。这一结论强调了量子优势在模拟热化动力学中的必要性,也为理解量子混沌系统的复杂性和计算难度提供了新的视角。

Abstract: We show that the emergence of a ``magic barrier'', characterized by a transient maximum of nonstabilizerness during rapid entanglement growth, is a universal feature of thermalization within quantum ergodic theories, which is intimately connected to random matrix spectral properties. Starting from a pure product state, we analytically determine the time evolution of entanglement entropy, antiflatness, capacity of entanglement, and nonlocal trace distance magic under unitary dynamics generated by random matrix Hamiltonians drawn from all three Gaussian ensembles. We find that the transient maxima of antiflatness, capacity of entanglement, and nonlocal magic occur on the same characteristic timescale as the maximum rate of entanglement growth. These results establish that the magic barrier is a universal feature of RMT thermalization, suggesting universality across quantum chaotic systems. We conclude that quantitative simulations of thermalization in ergodic quantum theories inherently require quantum computation.

</details>

Conclusion: 本研究成功将引力波尾部项的普适性从牛顿极限扩展到后牛顿展开的次领头阶，证明了尾部项系数的普适性在更高阶仍然成立——它们仅依赖于渐近轨迹而独立于相互作用细节。2→2散射的闭形式求和结果以及与相对论性猜想的一致性，增强了该理论框架的可靠性，为精确描述引力散射过程和引力波辐射提供了重要的理论基础。

Abstract: The low-frequency gravitational waveform emitted in a classical scattering process contains an infinite tower of late- and early-time tail terms of the form $u^{-r}(\ln|u|)^{r-1}$, $r \geq 1$. In the Newtonian limit, the coefficients of this entire tower have recently been shown to be universal, i.e. fixed by the asymptotic data of the scattering objects alone. At the retained order in the velocity expansion, these leading-logarithmic tails are determined to all orders in Newton's constant $G$. We extend this result to the first subleading order in the post-Newtonian expansion of the asymptotic states. We consider scattering of an arbitrary number of incoming and outgoing nonrelativistic objects interacting only through gravity at long range, and we derive the complete tower of tail terms at this order. The correction arises from the first subleading term in the retardation expansion and is determined by the asymptotic Newtonian trajectories of the massive particles. Its coefficients are again determined entirely by the asymptotic trajectories and are independent of the details of the interaction. For $2\to2$ scattering, the leading-logarithmic series can be summed in closed form. Our results agree with a relativistic conjecture proposed in earlier work arXiv:2610.07134.

</details>

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 传统的成对相互作用引力理论在描述多场引力波解时存在局限性。研究者希望在无鬼多引力理论框架下，探索超越成对相互作用的更一般的引力波解，以揭示多场相互作用所支持的新物理现象和精确解的可能性。

Method: 采用沿公共零方向形变成比例的AdS和闵可夫斯基解的方法，将完整的非线性场方程简化为波剖面的耦合线性微分方程，且不限制其振幅。利用与微扰Fierz-Pauli质量矩阵相同的本征基对系统进行对角化，将解分离为一个无质量模式和𝒩-1个有质量本征剖面。通过求解径向和横向解，施加AdS边界衰减条件，并通过局域平坦极限将AdS波与pp-波联系起来。

Result: 成功获得了无鬼多引力理论中AdS波和pp-波的精确解，包括显式的径向和横向解。边界衰减条件允许所有非负质量平方模式的适当径向分支，并排除负质量平方的剖面（包括Breitenlohner-Freedman阈值处的对数解）。通过具体例子展示了无法被无鬼成对理论复现的波构型，证实了多场相互作用的独特性。

Conclusion: 研究证明了无鬼多引力理论在超越成对相互作用时能够支持新的精确引力波解，这些解在传统成对相互作用理论中无法实现。通过对角化方法和边界条件分析，建立了AdS波与pp-波之间的联系，揭示了多场相互作用在引力波物理中的重要作用，为理解更复杂的引力理论提供了新的理论工具和精确解。

Abstract: We construct exact anti-de Sitter (AdS) waves and pp-waves in ghost-free multi-gravity beyond pairwise interactions for an arbitrary number $\mathcal{N}$ of vielbeins, in any spacetime dimension $D\geq3$. Deforming proportional AdS and Minkowski solutions along a common null direction reduces the full nonlinear field equations to coupled linear differential equations for the wave profiles, without restricting their amplitudes. The system is diagonalised in the same eigenbasis as the perturbative Fierz-Pauli mass matrix, separating the solutions into one massless and $\mathcal{N}-1$ massive eigenprofiles. We obtain explicit radial and transverse solutions and impose an AdS boundary falloff. This falloff admits the appropriate radial branch of every mode with non-negative squared mass and excludes profiles with negative squared masses, including logarithmic solutions at the Breitenlohner-Freedman threshold. We also relate the AdS waves to pp-waves through a local flat limit. Explicit examples exhibit wave configurations that cannot be reproduced by ghost-free pairwise theories, demonstrating that genuine multi-field interactions support new exact gravitational-wave solutions.

</details>

*Carolina Matté Gregory*

TL;DR: 本文研究了cohomogeneity-one $AdS_3\times M_8$系统的正则紧致解,其中$M_8$是三个常曲率黎曼曲面上的$S^2$丛。研究发现端点论证排除了不含正曲率因子的非平坦曲率符号扇区,而解析形变在所有剩余扇区中给出了具有四个径向形状参数的正则族。已知多项式解在这些族中构成真解析轨迹,非多项式性在参数空间中是普遍的。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 现有的Gauntlett等人提出的cohomogeneity-one $AdS_3\times M_8$系统中,已知多项式解的完备性和普遍性尚不明确。需要系统研究该系统的正则紧致解的存在性条件、参数空间结构,以及非多项式解的性质和物理意义,特别是在不同曲率符号扇区中解的分类和特征。

Method: 采用端点论证(endpoint argument)排除特定曲率符号扇区;使用解析形变技术构造具有四个径向形状参数的正则解族;建立四个磁通量比作为参考解附近的局部坐标系统;构造有理逆映射从有限通量比直接重构候选端点数据;在全正扇区中证明四维有限比邻域位于物理通量像内;分析磁周期和全纯中心荷的端点数据约化;验证等变局部化公式与端点结果的代数恒等性;量化非多项式修正并在显式收敛假设下分析有限退化。

Conclusion: 本研究完整刻画了cohomogeneity-one $AdS_3\times M_8$系统正则紧致解的参数空间结构,确立了曲率符号准则作为解存在性的充要条件(除完全平坦例外)。揭示了非多项式解的普遍性,已知多项式解仅为特殊情形。建立了通量比参数化与端点数据之间的明确对应关系,证明了全纯中心荷的等变局部化公式与端点结果的精确一致性,为该系统的物理理解和全息对偶理论提供了坚实的数学基础。

Abstract: We study regular compact solutions of the cohomogeneity-one $AdS_3\times M_8$ system of Gauntlett, Mac Conamhna, Mateos and Waldram, where $M_8$ is an $S^2$ bundle over three constant-curvature Riemann surfaces. We find that an endpoint argument rules out every non-flat curvature-sign sector without a positive-curvature factor, while an analytic deformation gives regular families with four radial shape parameters in all remaining sectors, with the base geometry held fixed. The known polynomial solutions form a proper analytic locus in these families. Non-polynomiality is therefore generic in parameter space, although the deviation from an endpoint-matching polynomial can be very small. Apart from the completely flat exception, the curvature-sign criterion is necessary and sufficient within this ansatz. Four magnetic flux ratios give local coordinates near the reference solution and its small-curvature deformations, and a rational inverse reconstructs candidate endpoint data directly from finite flux ratios. In the fully positive sector we prove that one explicit four-dimensional finite-ratio neighbourhood lies inside the physical flux image. The magnetic periods and leading holographic central charge reduce to endpoint data, and, once the common Brown--Henneaux normalization is fixed, the nontrivial flux dependence of the equivariant localization formula agrees with the endpoint result as an exact algebraic identity. We also quantify the non-polynomial corrections and analyse finite degenerations under explicit convergence hypotheses.

</details>
